Let's step back. The exercise: "The sensor's deployed rules miss most of the attack activity in this network." We need find activity deployed rules do not detect and write Suricata rules. (http.uri; content:"/zenworks/"; nocase; http.uri; content:"UploadServlet"; nocase; http.uri; content:"filename"; nocase; http.uri; content:".war"; nocase; ...)` But note URL-encoded `%3f` for `?` and `%3d` for `=`. The `http.uri` field may normalize `%3f` to `?` and `%3d` to `=`? Eve shows `.http.url` with `%3f`, `%3d`, `%7e` etc raw; `http.uri.raw` likely raw. We can match `/zenworks/UploadServlet` in `http.uri.raw` because the path part before `%3f` is `/zenworks/UploadServlet`; actually URL is `/zenworks/UploadServlet%3ffilename%3d../../webapps/CJMylKQ.war`, so raw contains `/zenworks/UploadServlet` and `filename` and `.war` but not `?`/`=`. `http.uri` normalized might decode to `/zenworks/UploadServlet?filename=../../webapps/CJMylKQ.war`. But to be safe, use `http.uri.raw; content:"/zenworks/UploadServlet"; nocase; content:"filename"; nocase; content:".war"; nocase;`. For second variant URL `/zenworks-fileupload/%3ffilename%3dk%7eo.ppt.war%26type%3d./../...` contains `/zenworks-fileupload/`, `filename`, `.war`. So one rule can match both with content `"/zenworks"` and `".war"` and `"filename"`? But that would also match any `/zenworks` with war and filename. Good for exploit. But maybe `http.uri.raw` contains `%3f`; content `"/zenworks"` matches both. We can do:
`alert http any any -> $HOME_NET any (msg:"EXPLOIT Novell ZENworks UploadServlet WAR upload attempt"; flow:to_server,established; http.uri.raw; content:"/zenworks"; nocase; http.uri.raw; content:"filename"; nocase; http.uri.raw; content:".war"; nocase; classtype:web-application-attack; sid:...)`
But Suricata sticky buffer can be repeated; content matches all in same raw URI buffer. This will fire on the two events. Good.

Could also detect the `zenworks-unmaninv` GET with `Filename=../../` (CVE-2010?) maybe remote file disclosure. Search.

[THINKING]

`/upload/upload?fileName=../../` — generic file upload traversal. Good for generic rule.

So a generic HTTP path traversal rule catches many. But maybe too broad; we can make it detect `/../` in URI raw. Let's evaluate false positives: raw `/../` could appear in legitimate requests, but in HTTP, clients usually normalize dot segments. However some web apps use dot segments intentionally? Path parameters `..` are not common. The existing Suricata rule "SURICATA HTTP URI terminated by non-compliant character" is not the same. I think a rule matching `content:"/../"` is acceptable but might be noisy. However the task wants rules that hold up on real network; "raw /../ in URI" is a classic traversal indicator and many IDS rules do exactly that (e.g., content:"../"). We can include.

--- Turn 93 | 8,593,548 in / 46,766 out ---

[AGENT TEXT]
## Coverage map

`count_by .event_type` over `logs/unsw-10-baseline/eve.json` gave the traffic shape:

- `flow` 38010, `dns` 26524, `anomaly` 22246, `ftp` 8811, `fileinfo` 8747, `http` 6721, `alert` 5063, `smtp` 3149, `ftp_data` 1092, `ssh` 775, `pop3` 260, `smb` 215, `dcerpc` 87, `sip` 47, `tftp` 16, `tls` 11, then small rfb/snmp/mqtt/ldap/dhcp/rdp counts.

`select(.event_type=="alert") | (.app_proto // "none")` showed alert coverage was very uneven:

- `dns`: 26524 events but only 7 alerts
- `ssh`: 775 events, 0 alerts
- `sip`: 47 events, 0 alerts
- `pop3`: 260 events, 1 alert
- `smtp`: 3149 events / 853 alerts, but 7893 `MIME_LONG_LINE` anomalies with no matching alert
- `smb`: 215 events / 185 alerts, but 14160 `duplicate_negotiate` anomalies and clear filename fuzzing with no matching alert
- `http`: broad coverage, but specific RFI / upload / traversal / XSS gaps remained

## Hunt and hypotheses

### DNS
`stack_values` on `select(.event_type=="dns") | .dns.queries[0].rrname` (rare) found `AUTHORS.BIND` (1) and `VERSION.BIND` (1) plus random high-entropy labels. Deployed rules alert `VERSION.BIND`, but `AUTHORS.BIND` had no signature. The events were external `175.45.176.x -> HOME_NET:53`. Hypothesis: BIND CHAOS-class info reconnaissance.

### HTTP
`stack_values` on `select(.event_type=="http") | .http.url` (rare) showed several distinct attack clusters:

- `/HPE/plugins/...?HPEinc=http://...` — 6 RFI attempts. `search_rules` found no HPEinc rule.
- `/zenworks/UploadServlet%3ffilename%3d...war` and `/zenworks-fileupload/...war` — 2 Novell ZENworks UploadServlet WAR upload attempts. `lookup_vulnerability` confirmed CVE-2010-5323/5324. No deployed rule.
- `/../`, `/...//...//autoexec.bat`, `/../../../../../boot.ini` — 13 raw path traversal requests. Deployed rules covered encoded variants but not raw `/../`.
- `/<img%20src=sa%20onerror=alert('Hello')>` — 1 raw XSS probe in URI; deployed XSS rules covered UA and encoded `<`, not this.

### SIP
`stack_values` on `select(.event_type=="sip") | .sip.uri` showed INVITE URI lengths up to 399 bytes. No SIP alerts existed. Hypothesis: SIP URI/overflow fuzz.

### TLS
`query_eve_json` on TLS events showed one certificate subject containing a null byte: `CN=lQReSa.com\u0000gMSDugk.com`. Hypothesis: null-byte certificate spoofing / malformed cert.

### SMTP
`select(.event_type=="smtp") | .smtp.rcpt_to` rare values showed `<aaaaaaaaaaa@aaaaa%s%s%n%n%n%s%s.com>`. Deployed rules had no SMTP RCPT format-string rule.

### SMB
SMB events showed external hosts issuing `SMB1_COMMAND_NT_CREATE_ANDX` with punctuation-only filenames like `(((((((((((((((((((((((((((((((` and `{{{{{{{{{{{{{{{{{{{{{{{{{{{{{{{{`. `smb.filename` was not a valid rule keyword in this Suricata build, so I used a raw TCP pcre over the UTF-16LE bytes.

## Rules saved

Appended to `rules/generated/unsw/rounds_hunt.rules`. All passed `test_rule` against `data/pcaps/unsw/10.pcap`.

| sid | Detection | test_rule result |
|---|---:|---|
| 7800017 | HP HPEinc RFI | PASS: 6 ext→int |
| 7800018 | Novell ZENworks UploadServlet WAR upload | PASS: 2 ext→int |
| 7800019 | raw HTTP `/../` path traversal | PASS: 13 ext→int |
| 7800020 | raw `<img ... onerror=` XSS in URI | PASS: 1 ext→int |
| 7800021 | DNS `AUTHORS.BIND` query | PASS: 1 ext→int |
| 7800022 | SIP INVITE with URI length ≥ 300 | PASS: 4 ext→int |
| 7800023 | TLS cert subject containing null byte | PASS: 1 int→ext |
| 7800024 | SMTP RCPT TO format string (`%n`) | PASS: 1 ext→int |
| 7800026 | SMB NT_CREATE_ANDX punctuation fuzz (raw UTF-16 pcre) | PASS: 12 ext→int |

All widened `any -> any` versions raised zero internal-to-internal alerts.

## Rules dropped / iterated

- **SIP `.{400,}`** — failed with 0 alerts because the longest observed URI was 399 bytes. Lowered to `.{300,}` and it passed.
- **TLS `tls.subject:"|00|"`** — invalid option; `tls.subject:"\x00"` and `"\u0000"` parsed but did not match. The working keyword was the `tls.cert_subject` sticky buffer with `content:"|00|"`.
- **SMB `smb.filename` sticky buffer** — unknown rule keyword in this build. A raw single-punctuation content rule (`(((`) caught only 1 variant; replaced with a raw pcre over the UTF-16LE bytes that caught 12 variants.
- **SMB single-punctuation raw rule** — passed but was too narrow; not saved in favor of the pcre rule.

## Attack-like activity I could not write a reliable rule for

- **DNS high-entropy labels** such as `YyG2K.8UV3kI7KM.MjK`, `pvp.8aAcaJ0N.suX`, `vxwiktyymwkj.com`: consistent with DNS tunneling/DGA fuzzing, but a generic high-entropy DNS-name rule is hard to make without high false positives.
- **DCE/RPC BIND fuzz on TCP 135** with random UUIDs and absurd interface versions like `62262.16695`: clearly malformed, but I found no reliable Suricata keyword for interface-version-only detection and did not want a broad raw-port rule.
- **SNMP community probing** (`ILMI`, `PRIVATE`): malicious-looking, but the deployed `PROTOCOL-SNMP request udp` rules already fire on the activity, so a new rule would be redundant.
- **POP3/IMAP/SMTP random-address spam/fuzz sessions**: much of it is suspicious but individually indistinguishable from generated lab traffic without an IP-pivot or high-noise heuristic.
